# Every assignment, by member

*Generated 2026-11-11 13:35 UTC from `channels/items.jsonl` — the append-only ledger, last row wins.*
1271 items on record; 757 review assignments ever made across 6 members; 514 items were never assigned to anybody.

## The legend

A letter says where an item stands **now**, not what happened to it first. `N+V = A+D+P+W+R` holds for the whole ledger at every moment.

| letter | what it means |
|---|---|
| **N** | performed, no review needed — only where the record says why |
| **V** | performed, needs review (the letter most items pass through) |
| **A** | accomplished — a model read it and kept it |
| **D** | delivered but unread — a machine found its paths on main; no model has opened it |
| **P** | postponed by a decision, with the reason on disk |
| **W** | waiting for review — nobody has looked at it yet |
| **R** | rejected, with the reason on disk |
| **—** | unassigned — no member has ever carried this item on a queue |

**V never appears in the item tables below**, and that is expected: V is the letter a filing is counted under while it is still undecided, and every item on record has since been decided one way or another. The tables show where each item stands now. If a V ever does appear in one of them, it means some item has been left undecided in a way this page did not expect — the letter scheme has changed — so the page would need to be re-read from the top rather than skimmed.

Two kinds of name exist. A **review** name says who must judge an item and write a verdict — that is what the member sections below list. A **reading** name says who must open an item a machine already closed; that is a queue, not a verdict, so it is kept in its own section at the end rather than double-counting every item.

**Every member section is grouped by the name the item was assigned to.** Where a name has ever been moved, the item appears under both — the original and the new one — so a re-draw cannot hide who had it first. On this page no item has ever carried two names, so the original assignment and the current one are the same name for every row.

## Reading assignments — delivered items nobody had read (separate queue)

A **reading name** is not a judgement of the work. When the machine that lands work on main sees that every file an item names is present, it closes the item itself, without any model reading it — that is letter D, and it is honest about what it is: delivered, unread. To stop those closures accumulating unseen, each one is given to a model to open. The name on it says whose job that is. Nothing about the work is being decided; somebody is being told to *look*. `A` below means the reader did open it and kept it; `D` means the name is still outstanding.
